classification:** Public **Dungeon range:** T1, Floors 1-10 **Primary source:** Haven Rangers’ Field Ledger and caravan-master kill reports **Reliability:** High **Encounter role:** Predator or pressure creature ## What It Is Cave Bear is a dungeon-adapted predator or hazard. Its behavior remains rooted in food, territory, nesting, and escape, which gives a prepared Expedition more options than a direct fight. ## What Experienced Expeditions Notice Scratches at the creature’s true height; prey dragged away rather than consumed in place; mineral dust in droppings; repeated avoidance of a seemingly safe route. Prey trails stop abruptly where the animal begins carrying rather than feeding. ## Creature Knowledge Use **Nature** unless the Director approves a more specific approach. | Result | Information Learned | |---|---| | DC 11 | Creature family, usual habitat, and dominant attack type. | | DC 15 | The first reliable counter and the sign that its strongest ability is ready. | | DC 19 | Its defining tactical weakness, safe harvest method, and likely retreat condition. | ## Knowledge Status and Reliability **Status:** Established Field Record **Primary source:** Haven Rangers’ Field Ledger and caravan-master kill reports **Reliability:** High **Last confirmed:** Current Opening and prior guild registries **Confirmed range:** Floors 1-10 **Rumored range:** Regional variants may extend beyond the confirmed range ## Stat Block **Large Beast, Unaligned** **Challenge** 2 (450 XP budget) · **Dungeon Range** T1 · **Floors** 1-10 **Armor** 13 · **Hit Points** 68 · **Speed** 40 ft. | STR | DEX | CON | INT | WIS | CHA | |---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:| | 16 (+3) | 14 (+2) | 13 (+1) | 3 (-4) | 12 (+1) | 6 (-2) | **Resistances** None **Immunities** None **Condition Immunities** None **Tactical Vulnerability** None identified **Save DC** 13 ### Traits **Territorial Morale.** It may retreat or break off if food, young, or escape access is secured. ### Actions **Multiattack.** The creature makes 2 attacks using the actions below. **Mauling Claws.** *Attack:* +5 to hit, one target. *Hit:* 1d10+2 slashing damage (average 8). ## How It Fights It opens with **Mauling Claws** against the target most capable of controlling its movement. It uses **Bite** to punish anyone who tries to stabilize that target. It attacks from concealment, protects its escape path, and stops pursuing once territory or food is secured. ## How to Defeat It **Prepare Before Contact** - Prepare resistance or mitigation for slashing, piercing damage. - Carry bait, scent control, and a noncombat retreat route. **Combat Priority** Control its escape and feeding choices rather than cornering it. **Exploit** The defining weakness is **None identified**. Treat this as an encounter solution rather than an automatic damage vulnerability. Successful Research, Recon, or Resource actions should make the exploit easier or safer. **Withdraw When** Withdraw or change the objective when the creature reaches favorable terrain or isolates a target. A failed retreat against this creature usually costs more than abandoning the immediate treasure. ## Habitat, Goals, and Lair Predators choose terrain that hides approach and protects retreat. The lair is usually more dangerous than the animal. Not every dangerous creature serves a faction or cosmic principle. Ordinary predators become expedition-level threats when dungeon terrain, repeated Openings, or magical food chains reshape them. Animal Handling, food, territory, and nonthreatening movement matter more than language. ## Treasure and Harvest **Harvest skill:** Nature · **Harvest DC:** 10 · **Base time:** 30 minutes | Component | Tier | Drop / Value | |---|---:|---| | Bear meat | 1 | 54%, 12 gp base | | Bear bone shard | 2 | 45%, 24 gp base | **Known recipe links:** Greater Bear Bone Shard Stew. Hide, meat, bone, venom, teeth, and organs are practical resources. Damage type and preservation method determine quality. ## Variants and Encounter Use Change hunger, territory, age, injury, and nesting condition. Environmental behavior is the primary variant system. --- *Compiled from the cited expedition records. It resolves specific conflicts between approved Core V2, compatible Player Rulebook V6 rules and the retained Gameplay Loop. Original books and alternate procedures remain unchanged. Full conditions, equipment, hazards and recovery integration remain separate work. Use with [class rules](CLASS_PLAYTEST_RULES.md), [spell rules](SPELL_INTEGRATION_RULES.md) and the [complete coverage ledger](GAME_DEVELOPMENT_COVERAGE.md). The [source conference](../../../../docs/audit/2026-10-08/full-game/CODEX_CORE_RESOLUTION_CONFERENCE.json), [V6 findings](../../../../docs/audit/2026-10-08/full-game/CODEX_CORE_SOURCE_FOLLOWUP.md) and [GPT proposal](../../../../docs/audit/2026-10-08/full-game/GPT_CORE_STATE_AND_INJURY_PROPOSAL.md) preserve the reasoning. ## Which rule applies The domain authority is [Core Systems Known Descent V2](../../../../books/player/Decennium_Descent_Core_Systems_Bible_v2_Known_Descent.docx). The approved [Player Rulebook V6](../../../../books/player/Decennium_Descent_Player_Rulebook_v6.docx) supplies compatible rules that Core does not replace. This overlay states the selected interpretation or newly authored rule wherever those sources leave a gap. An explicit exception on an operative, reviewed class/spell/item card applies only within its printed scope; a historical alternate or a same-named feature is not an extra grant. This candidate does not promote an entire historical branch. | Question | Active rule and provenance | Inactive alternate | |---|---|---| | Attacks | Core V2: meet AC; natural 1 misses; natural 20 hits and doubles damage dice. | Applying the general Gameplay four-degree roll to attacks. | | Task checks | Explicitly adopted Gameplay four degrees and natural shifts. | Assuming every critical result creates an unprinted effect. | | Ordinary saves | Binary meet-DC test; printed failure margins remain literal. Natural 1/20 do not generically shift saves in this candidate. This separation is an integration choice. | Automatic critical saves or natural-result shifts inferred from the task rule. | | Death-save timing | Core and V6: start of turn; regular-turn-only timing is a provisional guard. | Gameplay end-of-turn saves. | | Wounds | Core trigger/save, Constitution-save and Hit-Dice-recovery penalties, and three-Wound Critical state. | Gameplay automatic Wound triggers, Hit Dice healing penalty, half Speed and Strain lock. | | Named Wounded condition | Core paragraph 782 expressly describes the same Wound count, Constitution penalty, Hit Dice recovery and three-Wound Critical state. Apply those records once. | V6 Appendix A paragraphs 9971–9974 give a conflicting temporary condition cleared by magic/rest with Constitution-save disadvantage; Core replaces it here. | | Stable, instant death, knockout and temporary HP | Compatible recovered V6 rules. | Treating these recovered rules as newly invented. | | Ordinary Wound treatment | Explicit Gameplay-derived integration below. | Claiming it is original Core wording or ordinary HP healing. | | Injury table and unresolved thresholds | New provisional choices below, with their search limits stated. | Dropping Core's failure-by-5 consequence because its table was not located. | ## Rolls Choose the roll type before rolling. Use the actual ability modifier, including negative values, and normal proficiency only when applicable. Existing advantage/disadvantage chooses the higher/lower of two d20s; multiple sources do not stack, and any advantage plus disadvantage cancels. Add other legal modifiers once. Fractions round down unless a rule specifies otherwise. One tile is 5 feet. Class L, S and effect M retain their definitions in the shared class rules. **Attack:** roll d20 plus the appropriate attack modifier against AC. Meeting AC hits. Natural 1 always misses; natural 20 always hits and critically doubles damage dice, not flat modifiers. Beating AC by 10 is not a critical hit. An explicit broader critical range does not stack with another; use the broadest eligible range. In this overlay, a natural result below 20 in that range must still meet AC unless the feature expressly says it always hits. **Task, skill or tool check against a fixed DC:** total at least DC+10 is Critical Success; at least DC is Success; at most DC-10 is Critical Failure; other totals below DC are Failure. Natural 20 then improves one degree and natural 1 worsens one, bounded at either end. A task printing only success/failure collapses the two upper degrees to success and two lower degrees to failure; no extra consequence is invented. A natural 20 can still fail an otherwise impossible check, and a natural 1 with sufficient modifiers can still succeed. **Saving throw:** total at least DC succeeds; otherwise it fails. Natural 1 and 20 have no generic special outcome. A printed failure by 5 or 10 compares the final total with the DC; it does not add an unprinted critical-save system. Death saves are the specific exception below. **Opposed check, new provisional tie rule:** compare final totals. Higher wins; an exact tie leaves the existing state unchanged, so the initiating creature fails to change it. Do not turn task natural shifts into hidden numeric modifiers. A maneuver explicitly printing degree outcomes must specify how those apply. ## Resolve a damage event Resolve each genuinely separate hit or damage event once. Do not split one packet to multiply Wound saves or combine separate hits merely to cross a threshold. Feature-specific prevention that acts before damage or before reaching zero resolves at its printed timing first. 1. Resolve attack/save, damage and applicable defenses. The remaining nonnegative amount is **resolved damage**. 2. Record HP, Wounds and zero-HP state before the event. Remove temporary HP first. **Post-temp damage = resolved damage minus temporary HP absorbed.** Keep this amount even when it exceeds current HP; floor the resulting current HP at zero. 3. Check V6 Instant Death. If post-temp damage minus HP before the hit is at least maximum HP, the creature dies. For a creature already at zero, this is post-temp damage at least maximum HP. Stop: there is no Wound save, death-save failure or successful knockout from that event. 4. Otherwise, a living creature makes one Wound save if it dropped from positive HP to zero, if **2 × post-temp damage is at least maximum HP**, or if the effect actually inflicts Maiming. Overlapping triggers still cause only one save. The post-temp threshold and one-save rule are new provisional interpretations. Maiming is an independent trigger even when temporary HP absorbs all ordinary damage; immunity to Maiming itself still applies. 5. Make a Constitution save against **max(10, floor(post-temp damage / 2))**. Use pre-event Wounds for the -1-per-Wound penalty. Maiming with zero post-temp damage uses DC 10. A Wound gained now cannot penalize the roll that created it. 6. A success adds no Wound. A failure that began with zero, one or two Wounds adds exactly one; failure by 5 or more also gives that Wound one Injury. A failure that began with three Wounds kills the creature instead: no fourth Wound and no Injury roll. Stop processing later living-target consequences if dead. 7. If the creature was already at zero before this event and post-temp damage is positive, end Stable. A creature using death saves adds one failure, or two for a critical hit; three failures kill it. An ordinary creature that does not use death saves dies instead, without starting death counters. A hit from positive HP to zero does not also cause a same-packet death failure. 8. If this event newly reduces positive HP to zero, a valid melee knockout makes the surviving creature Stable. Otherwise a character/significant NPC using death saves begins dying; an ordinary monster dies. A previously Stable creature whose temporary HP absorbed all damage remains Stable unless an independent Maiming result killed it. The Director identifies significant NPCs consistently. Knockout cannot override Instant Death or a fatal Wound save. Core's Maiming and Wound trigger rules remain subject to an actual feature's eligibility. The reference helper receives already legal events; it does not grant every attack Maiming or a damage-prevention exception. ## Zero HP and recovery A living character at zero HP is Unconscious. Core's condition already includes Incapacitated, unawareness, dropping held items and falling Prone; an adjacent hit is critical as specified there. Healing does not silently remove other independent causes of Unconscious or Prone. While dying, make one death save at the **start of each regular initiative turn**. Roll d20 without modifiers unless a feature specifically modifies death saves. Natural 20 restores 1 HP; natural 1 adds two failures. Other results of 10 or more add one success; 9 or less add one failure. Three successes make the creature Stable; three failures make it Dead. Extra turns and Actions add no extra death saves. Stable or Dead creatures do not roll them. Regaining positive HP or becoming Stable resets both counters. Temporary HP does not raise normal HP, end Unconscious or reset counters. Fully absorbed damage does not end Stable or add a death failure in this provisional interpretation. Independent Maiming can still cause its Wound save. **Stabilize:** an Action and DC 10 Wisdom (Medicine) check, or an Action using one healer's-kit use automatically, stabilizes a living dying creature at zero. It remains Unconscious and makes no death saves. Roll 1d4 once when it becomes Stable and record its absolute recovery time that many hours later. If still Stable then, it regains 1 HP. Damage reaching normal HP cancels that deadline and resumes dying with the appropriate failures. Rest and Stable recovery use the same elapsed clock, so four hours of recovery inside an eight-hour Watch do not cost twelve hours. A later rest does not separately replay the Stable healing event. **Dead:** ordinary healing and stabilization do not restore life. Resurrection must satisfy its own willing-soul, body, time, cost and target rules. It does not automatically remove Wounds, Corruption, debts, legal death or claims upon the body. Corpses cannot supply repeated resource triggers. ## Wounds and Critical state Each Wound imposes **-1 Constitution saves** and reduces the normal maximum number of Hit Dice recovered per long rest by one, to a minimum of zero. It does not reduce each healing die. The broader recovery rules own the normal recovery maximum and rest legality. At three Wounds the creature is **Critically Wounded**: Speed is reduced by 10 feet, to minimum zero, and the next failed Wound save is fatal. The failure that first raises two Wounds to three is not that later failure. Core's named **Wounded** condition (paragraph 782, preserved in [the 28-condition source register](../../data/playtest/core_condition_sources.json)) explicitly summarizes these same Wounds; it does not add duplicate penalties. Its conflicting V6 predecessor is inactive in this overlay. This individual source comparison does not complete the remaining condition audit. **New provisional healing ceiling:** while Critical, healing cannot increase HP above **max(1, maximum HP - 1)**. Maximum HP itself does not change. Gaining the third Wound does not immediately remove existing HP; a later attempted heal also cannot reduce HP. The minimum of one permits recovery when maximum HP is one and is an explicit exception to “cannot regain full HP.” Removing enough Wounds to leave Critical removes its Speed penalty and ceiling, but does not itself heal HP. ## Linked injuries The inspected eight approved books and current text sources reference an Injury table, but no actual table was located. The full Wound/Medicine searches of both Core editions found no alternate general treatment procedure. Those are search limits, not proof about every archive. This **new provisional d6 table** supplies Core's failure-by-5 consequence until a reviewed replacement is adopted. | d6 | Injury | Exact penalty | |---:|---|---| | 1 | Concussion | -1 Initiative and Perception checks. | | 2 | Offensive Trauma | -1 attack rolls. | | 3 | Mobility Trauma | All non-teleport Speeds reduced by 5 feet, minimum zero. | | 4 | Fine-Motor Trauma | -1 tool checks and Dexterity checks primarily manipulating an object, mechanism, lock or device. | | 5 | Exertion Trauma | -1 Strength and Dexterity ability checks. | | 6 | Cognitive Trauma | -1 Intelligence, Wisdom and Charisma ability checks. | Link each result to the one Wound just gained. An Injury never adds another Wound. Record duplicate results separately, but identical penalties apply once. Different Injury penalties affecting the same roll or stat also use only the strongest Injury penalty. V6 paragraph 7925 explicitly calls Initiative a Dexterity check, so both Concussion and Exertion Trauma can apply, with only one -1 Injury penalty. An injury affects future Initiative rolls, never an order already established. This does not change other Initiative procedures. Mobility Trauma can coexist with the separate Core Critical Speed penalty, applying both once. Other effects keep their own stacking rules. Removing a Wound ends its linked Injury. A duplicate penalty persists while any remaining Wound carries that result. When a removal effect names no specific Wound, the patient chooses existing records. Injuries cause no extra damage, Actions lost, disadvantage, maximum-HP reduction or resource triggers. Describe equivalent trauma for unusual anatomy while retaining the printed penalty. ## Ordinary Wound treatment This is an explicit Gameplay-derived integration. A living patient with at least one Wound receives eight elapsed hours of treatment with a medical kit and **one Supply**. **New provisional cost decision:** commit and consume that Supply once when treatment begins, on every attempt, even on success or interruption; no extra Supply is charged for failure. The retained Gameplay text requires one Supply and explicitly mentions consumption on failure but does not clearly settle success refunds. This candidate deliberately settles the ambiguity as a cost on every attempt, rather than claiming that interpretation is recovered source wording. Choose the Wound being treated before beginning. At completion roll Wisdom (Medicine) against the applicable tier DC: I 12, II 13, III 15, IV 16, V 18, VI 19. Unverified higher-tier procedures remain with the later Director/hazard pass. Critical Success removes the chosen Wound and one other existing Wound chosen by the patient, if available. Success removes the chosen Wound. Failure removes none. Critical Failure removes none and gives the **patient** one Strain; identifying the patient is a new clarification. Associated Injuries end with removed Wounds. Treatment itself does not restore HP. Whether treatment qualifies as a legal rest is determined separately; the active healer cannot automatically claim an eight-hour Rest Watch while treating. Count the eight hours once, with any Stable recovery occurring on the same clock. ## Verification scope `tools/core_playtest_rules.py` is a reference for roll types and the damage/Wound/death state transitions. It does not select legal Actions or targets, run all class reactions, roll damage, apply every defense, adjudicate all conditions, run medical-kit/Supply inventories or simulate a campaign clock. Its callers supply legal events and actual elapsed time. `tools/validate_core_playtests.py` reports named regression cases and sampled state invariants. Passing checks do not establish human balance or completion of the remaining game systems. Generic Core Bleeding and Burning join family.bleeding and family.ongoing-fire respectively, with source-owner stack groups. At one identical target timing marker, all due generic and custom groups in that family compete: highest expected unmitigated damage, then earliest application. Only the winning group's source-permitted instances deal damage. All due groups consume that tick; losers do not defer or bank it. Different printed markers resolve separately. No record also receives a duplicate generic tick. Core generic timing is end of regular target turn; extra turns do not tick. Outside initiative preserve one periodic step per complete six seconds with no duplicate interval across initiative transitions. Strain is not globally unique: expedition.strain and warrior.serum-strain are separate source-qualified tracks, both distinct from the six-level Exhaustion condition. See the Strain namespace reference for the current candidate. Oracle one-turn Revelation backlash is also separate from global Exhaustion; its printed penalties and endpoint remain unchanged. Wound Eater feeding failures explicitly use global Exhaustion. Never silently convert similarly named tracks. See CONDITION_RECOVERY_PLAYTEST.md for finite recovery, cleansing, substance, Intoxication and Unity procedures. These new proposals are labeled separately from recovered source rules. Conditions do not reveal Director secrets or force player beliefs or decisions. SUPERSESSION — NEW PROVISIONAL INTEGRATION: The unified Bleeding/ongoing-fire family registry supersedes earlier provisional clauses that selected generic records separately, used ending DC as a damage tie-breaker, cleared all generic Bleeding through one Medicine result, or used one Burning save to clear all generic Burning. Original Core quotations and raw review history remain unchanged. See CUSTOM_CONDITION_BINDINGS.md and the recorded GPT_GENERIC_CUSTOM_ALIGNMENT review. ## Quick index | Condition | Configuration | |---|---| | [Addicted](#addicted) | active provisional overlay | | [Bleeding](#bleeding) | active provisional overlay | | [Blinded](#blinded) | active provisional overlay | | [Burning](#burning) | active provisional overlay | | [Charmed](#charmed) | active provisional overlay | | [Corrupted](#corrupted) | active provisional overlay | | [Dazed](#dazed) | active provisional overlay | | [Deafened](#deafened) | active provisional overlay | | [Exhausted](#exhausted) | active provisional overlay | | [Frightened](#frightened) | active provisional overlay | | [Grappled](#grappled) | active provisional overlay | | [Incapacitated](#incapacitated) | active provisional overlay | | [Intoxicated](#intoxicated) | active provisional overlay | | [Invisible](#invisible) | active provisional overlay | | [Marked](#marked) | active provisional overlay | | [Paralyzed](#paralyzed) | active provisional overlay | | [Petrified](#petrified) | active provisional overlay | | [Poisoned](#poisoned) | active provisional overlay | | [Prone](#prone) | active provisional overlay | | [Resonance Burn](#resonance-burn) | preserved; inactive in this Dissonance configuration | | [Restrained](#restrained) | active provisional overlay | | [Silenced](#silenced) | active provisional overlay | | [Staggered](#staggered) | active provisional overlay | | [Stunned](#stunned) | active provisional overlay | | [Unconscious](#unconscious) | active provisional overlay | | [Unity Exposure](#unity-exposure) | active provisional overlay | | [Withdrawal](#withdrawal) | active provisional overlay | | [Wounded](#wounded) | active provisional overlay | <a id=\"addicted\"></a> ---CONDITIONS RANGE--- ## Grappled **Preserved Core text:** Speed 0; ends if grappler is incapacitated or reach is broken. **Effect:** Speed becomes 0 and cannot be increased by bonuses while Grappled. **Application:** A Grappled application records the grappler/source and its reach. **Duration and ending:** Ends if the grappler is Incapacitated, reach breaks, or the applying effect ends/its escape succeeds. A class/spell card with a fixed save DC uses an Action and STR (Athletics) or DEX (Acrobatics) against that DC. An ordinary V6 grapple uses an Action to escape with STR (Athletics) or DEX (Acrobatics) opposed by the grappler's STR (Athletics); higher total wins, ties retain the grapple. Multiple grapplers require ending each relationship. **Stacking:** Multiple grapplers may maintain separate Grappled records; Speed remains 0 until all relevant grapples end. **Immunities and specific exceptions:** A creature immune to Grappled cannot receive it. Teleportation or forced movement that breaks reach ends that grapple unless a specific effect says otherwise. **Compatible V6/source details:** - V6 adds that the creature cannot benefit from bonuses to Speed and confirms ending when the grappler is incapacitated or an effect removes the target from reach. - V6 all-XML paragraph 8206 gives ordinary grapple application and opposed escape; fixed-DC card escapes remain specific class/spell rules. **Remaining wider integration work:** - Final generic grapple/shove procedure remains part of the combat maneuver audit. Provenance: Core V2 `books/player/Decennium_Descent_Core_Systems_Bible_v2_Known_Descent.docx`, zero-based all-XML heading paragraph 747, definition paragraph 748; both positions, exact text and book SHA-256 are verified against the approved book and retained in the [condition data](../../data/playtest/condition_playtests.json). <a id=\"incapacitated\"></a> ## Incapacitated **Preserved Core text:** Cannot take actions or reactions. **Effect:** The creature cannot take an Action, Bonus Action or Reaction. Passive effects continue unless their own rules require consciousness or another condition disables them. Incapacitated by itself does not reduce Speed or prohibit speech. **Application:** Applied by a condition or effect that explicitly says the creature is Incapacitated. **Duration and ending:** Use the parent condition/effect's duration. **Stacking:** Does not stack. **Immunities and specific exceptions:** Current shared concentration rules end concentration immediately when the creature becomes Incapacitated. A specific effect may preserve a passive benefit through Incapacitation if explicitly stated. **Compatible V6/source details:** - V6 matches: cannot take actions or reactions. **Explicit integration decisions:** - NEW integration: Bonus Actions are included in unavailable actions. - Already explicit in CLASS_PLAYTEST_RULES.md: becoming Incapacitated ends concentration. Provenance: Core V2 `books/player/Decennium_Descent_Core_Systems_Bible_v2_Known_Descent.docx`, zero-based all-XML heading paragraph 749, definition paragraph 750; both positions, exact text and book SHA-256 are verified against the approved book and retained in the [condition data](../../data/playtest/condition_playtests.json). <a id=\"intoxicated\"></a> ---CONDITIONS RANGE--- ## Prone **Preserved Core text:** Crawl or stand; melee attackers within 5 ft. have advantage; ranged attackers have disadvantage. **Effect:** Own attacks have disadvantage. Melee attacks against it within 5 feet have advantage; ranged attacks against it have disadvantage at every distance; other attacks beyond 5 feet have disadvantage. Movement requires crawling or standing. Drop Prone without Speed expenditure; standing costs half current Speed, rounded down, requires nonzero Speed and sufficient movement. Crawl costs 2 feet per foot, or 3 in difficult terrain. **Application:** Applied when an effect explicitly knocks a creature Prone or another rule such as Unconscious causes it. **Duration and ending:** Ends when the creature successfully stands or an explicit effect ends Prone. **Stacking:** Does not stack. **Immunities and specific exceptions:** Prone immunity blocks application. A feature may explicitly ignore Prone attack penalties. **Compatible V6/source details:** - V6: only movement is crawl unless standing; own attack rolls have disadvantage; attacks from within 5 feet have advantage, otherwise disadvantage. **Source conflicts:** - For a ranged attack made from within 5 feet, V6 distance-only wording would grant advantage while Core specifically says ranged attackers have disadvantage. Core controls. **Explicit integration decisions:** - Core-vs-V6 conflict resolved in favor of Core for ranged attacks within 5 feet. - Recovered V6 movement: half-Speed standing and 2/3-for-1 crawling. NEW explicit zero-Speed standing prohibition, preventing zero-cost standing while Grappled. Provenance: Core V2 `books/player/Decennium_Descent_Core_Systems_Bible_v2_Known_Descent.docx`, zero-based all-XML heading paragraph 763, definition paragraph 764; both positions, exact text and book SHA-256 are verified against the approved book and retained in the [condition data](../../data/playtest/condition_playtests.json). <a id=\"resonance-burn\"></a> ---CONDITIONS RANGE--- ## Restrained **Preserved Core text:** Speed 0; attacks against have advantage, own attacks and Dexterity saves have disadvantage. **Effect:** Speed becomes 0 and cannot benefit from Speed bonuses. Attack rolls against the creature have advantage; its own attack rolls and Dexterity saving throws have disadvantage. **Application:** Only an effect explicitly inflicting Restrained applies it. **Duration and ending:** Use the applying effect's printed duration, save or escape rule. If an effect creates a physical restraint and provides no escape procedure, use the existing shared playtest escape procedure only if that source is eligible for it. **Stacking:** Multiple Restrained applications do not compound penalties, but all independently maintained sources must end before the creature is no longer Restrained. **Immunities and specific exceptions:** Restrained immunity blocks application. Teleportation does not automatically end a restraint unless it breaks the source relationship or the applying effect says so. **Compatible V6/source details:** - V6 adds that the creature cannot benefit from bonuses to Speed. **Remaining wider integration work:** - Final generic physical-restraint escape procedure in combat rules. Provenance: Core V2 `books/player/Decennium_Descent_Core_Systems_Bible_v2_Known_Descent.docx`, zero-based all-XML heading paragraph 767, definition paragraph 768; both positions, exact text and book SHA-256 are verified against the approved book and retained in the [condition data](../../data/playtest/condition_playtests.json). <a id=\"silenced\"></a> ---LIFE--- # Healing, survival and resurrection — playtest interface **REVIEWED / reference-VALIDATED — selected scope only.** The [final notebook review](../../../../docs/audit/2026-10-08/full-game/GPT_HEALING_FINAL_CLOSURE.md) closes147 classified class passages plus6 individually reviewed ordinary spell objects. Those6 spells are now operational playtest drafts; the catalog has78 operational and770 unfinished entries. This is a bounded interface, not DD24 completion:131 additional semantic matches and healing content in other domains still need dispositions. Original cards/books are preserved. [Exact source procedures](HEALING_DEATH_SOURCE_BINDINGS.md) · [Latest boundary decisions](../../../../docs/audit/2026-10-08/full-game/GPT_HEALING_FINAL_BOUNDARIES.md) · [Supplement decisions](../../../../docs/audit/2026-10-08/full-game/GPT_HEALING_SUPPLEMENT_REVIEW.md) · [Reference checks](HEALING_PLAYTEST_VALIDATION.json) · [Source integrity](HEALING_DEATH_SOURCE_VALIDATION.json) ## Distinct life operations | Operation | Result | Restriction interface | |---|---|---| | Minimum-HP prevention | Preserve positiveHP instead of crossing a floor | Not HP gain; cannot create positiveHP from0 | | Ordinary healing | Increase a living creature's normalHP | Apply modifiers, prohibitions, reserve and Critical ceiling | | Direct state recovery | Specifically defined dying/Stable/HP recovery | Broad cannot-regain-HP applies; ordinary healing modifiers do not | | Would-die replacement | Replace a pending death before it becomes final | Exact source eligibility; not a generic damage floor | | Resurrection | Actually dead willing identity returns at printedHP | Separate life transition; actual corpse/soul blockers apply | | Companion reconstruction | Restore a device/companion through its own source | No general creature resurrection permission | | TemporaryHP | Separate nonstacking buffer | Not healing, stabilization or resurrection | Names never select the operation. Phoenix Rebirth heals a still-living ally at0. Cleric Resurrection returns an actual dead identity. Ritual of Undeath's Ward replaces death; ordinary Death Ward is a positive-to-zero floor. Feign death and spirit manifestations are not death or resurrection. ## Damage quantities and resolution | Quantity | Definition and use | |---|---| | resolvedDamage | After defenses, before tempHP; positive damage event iff greater than0 | | tempAbsorbed | min(tempHP, resolvedDamage) | | postTempDamage | resolvedDamage minus tempAbsorbed, uncapped by currentHP; Core Instant Death/Wounds | | actualHpDamage | NormalHP lost to the packet after true prevention; recovery and state-assignment loss excluded | | actualDamageDealt | NEW anti-overkill bridge: tempAbsorbed plus actualHpDamage; numeric actual-damage drains/reflections | Ordinary “damages,” “takes damage” and “damaging hit” predicates use a positive resolved packet. Actual-HP wording (Burning Blood/Rewind Wound) uses actualHpDamage. Numeric actual-damage wording (Sanguine Rite/Echo Leech/Merciful Smite/Wound Eater/Gravebrand) uses actualDamageDealt. Source attack/hit predicates remain distinct. Example: HP10/temp10 takes5: numeric actual damage5, actualHP damage0, positive packet. HP1 under a floor takes5: numeric0 but positive packet. HP3 takes12: numeric3, raw post-temp12. HP0 takes1: numeric0, positive packet and normal death-failure consequences. Divine Judgment consumes its once-this-actual-target-turn entitlement on a positive packet, then reflects min(Cleric level, max(1, floor(actualDamageDealt/2))). Thus HP1-floor/numeric0 reflects1 and consumes; fully prevented resolved0 does neither. Wrathful Brand/Balanced Scales use the positive predicate where their source says damage occurred. Resolve a damage event in this order: 1. Snapshot pre-event HP/tempHP/Wounds; resolve defenses, temp absorption and uncapped post-temp damage. 2. Core Instant Death uses postTempDamage minus preHP greater than or equal to effective maximum when reduced to0. It precedes ordinary floors. Open a legal would-die replacement; stop that packet's death procedure. No Wound save or zero-HP failure follows this branch. 3. If not Instant Death, resolve applicable true floors. They cannot raise an already0 creature. They preserve raw damage and attached conditions; a prevented drop creates no drop-only Wound trigger, but half-max/Maiming still applies. 4. Spiral-TouchedIII alone has a specific pre-Wound recovery exception. If positive-to-zero remains, spend its use, preserve that raw crossing trigger, attempt quarter-max direct recovery and apply phase-out. Broad HP-gain prohibition blocks the recovery but not the use/phase. 5. Resolve the one Core Wound save using original post-temp damage and pre-event Wounds. Fatal Wound death opens its own legal replacement window. Actual death ends Spiral phase; a successful independent Ward prevents actual death. 6. Resolve already-zero damage failures/death and its legal replacement window. A successful replacement stops skipped same-packet death branches; never replay a Wound/failure branch merely because replacement changedHP. 7. Finalize a newly surviving0 creature's dying/Stable/knockout state. Only an explicitly triggered post-zero Reaction such as Grace Reversal can act before ordinary zeroHP Reaction loss finalizes. 8. Resolve eligible post-zero Phoenix/Unyielding-style healing only if target still lives, followed by later triggers. This healing never retroactively reduces the triggering packet's numeric damage. The affected creature chooses among simultaneously eligible offered survival effects, then rechecks remaining triggers. A paid Reaction spends its declared cost; a limited automatic survival use spends when its actual source resolves. A true floor preventing the crossing means Spiral's use is not spent. No generic extra Reaction or return from death is created. For Ritual Ward, sufficient body and no explicitly unpreventable death are prerequisites. It can intercept Instant Death, fatal Wounds, third death-save failure, eligible direct death and lethal tracks. Positive pre-eventHP→1 is loss prevention and works through Decay. Already0 with HP gain prohibited instead becomes living Stable0: clear counters and establish a d4-hour recovery deadline immediately. Freeze packetHpLossBeforeReplacement after true floors but before Ward. Ward metrics are min(packetHpLossBeforeReplacement, max(0, preHP minus replacementHP)); never include loss caused solely by assigning a lowerHP state. HP20 takes10 then fatal Wound/Ward1 means actualHP damage10, not19. HP5 takes100 then Ward1 means actualHP damage4, raw100. Direct death (e.g. Wound EaterIII) produces no damage or Wound save. Its healing requires actual death; a Ward interception prevents that trigger without refunding the paid activation. False LifeI has only its printed floor and failed-death-save replacement modes sharing1/LR; it is not an all-causes Ward. ## ZeroHP permissions and start timing A conscious but dying creature still rolls at each regular start unless Stable. Extra turns do not advance these regular-start deadlines. Further damage and actual death retain normal consequences. Mage Undying Spellform partially overrides only zeroHP Unconscious/Incapacitated restrictions: normal speech, voluntary half-Speed movement, normal Action, legal spellcasting with that Action, and starting/maintaining concentration. No independent BA/Reaction. Existing concentration avoids automatic loss from zeroHP, but the triggering damage still requires its concentration save. Separate Stunned/Unconscious/Expedition5 or6/Silence and other restrictions still apply. Healing above0 ends the feature. Expiry at0 destabilizes, cancels the Stable recovery deadline and adds one failure; the third kills normally and permits only an eligible would-die replacement. Oracle False LifeIII permits exactly one choice during the allowed zeroHP turn: move up to Speed, speak, or activate one Revelation. The latter uses its printed Action/BA/trigger (trigger must occur on that turn), costs and components. It can start concentration; the feature did not preserve earlier concentration. No independent actions accompany the choice. False LifeV quarter-max direct recovery resolves at its next regular start before the death save. Blocked recovery leaves0 without invented stability; dead creatures do not revive. Spiral phase expires at its next regular start before normal start processing. Fate-BoundIV rerolls a failed death save before committing natural1/counters; use the new result. Its generic death-effect save bonus is not an ordinary death-save modifier. A natural20 whose1HP is blocked uses the previously reviewed Decay bridge: Stable0, cleared counters, immediate d4-hour deadline and preserved used-turn marker. It does not spend Ward. This differs from blocked False LifeV, which remains dying. ## Ordinary healing arithmetic BODY HEADINGS 1: # Bodies equipment and carrying 5: ## Entering and changing forms 22: ## What melds 30: ## Using equipment in a form 43: ## Returning equipment 57: ## Placement and involuntary endings 78: ## Body weight and conditions 88: ## Carrying and Load 113: ## Meld limits across repeated transformations 129: ## Extraction Class and Secure Cargo 147: ## Examples 161: ## Source decisions and implementation status"

Highest first. Players choose player/player ties; the Director resolves ties involving NPCs. Keep the resulting ordered slots for the encounter. A round represents approximately six seconds. A **regular turn** is a creature's normal initiative slot even if it cannot act. An extra turn is distinct. 30: 31: A turn normally permits movement, one Action, a Bonus Action when an actual rule grants a use, and one ordinary free object interaction. A second ordinary interaction or an Action-time object activation uses an Action. Brief communication is not an extra Action. Conditions and specific features may restrict these permissions. 32: 33: The shared limit is one **extra full Action per creature per numbered round**. Such an Action cannot grant another extra Action, Bonus Action or Reaction. This cap is independent of Reaction timing. Extra turns do not by themselves refresh Reactions, recharge, periodic ticks, recovery ticks or the regular-turn allowances defined here. 34: 35: **Reaction interpretation.** Begin an encounter with one available Reaction unless surprised or otherwise prohibited. Refresh at each regular own-turn start; never at the numbered round boundary or an extra turn. A late-initiative creature can therefore react before its first turn, refresh, and react again in the same numbered round. It cannot react until that turn ends. Its slot, durations, passive effects, concentration, periodic effects and recharge still occur. Surprise alone is not Incapacitated or Unconscious. Brief communication and an otherwise legal simple free interaction remain possible. 40: 41: **Entry — NEW.** Standard end-round reinforcements roll/receive initiative and first act next round. An explicit midround entrant acts this round only if its complete ordered slot is still upcoming; entry during that exact slot counts as already passed. It has its initial Reaction unless prohibited. A summon-specific timing rule overrides this generic entry procedure. 42: 43: After the final slot, resolve Core's end-round hazards, reinforcements, morale, objectives and boss phases. Conclude the scene with surrender, capture, pursuit, harvesting access and noise consequences under their own rules; winning initiative does not resolve those automatically. 44: 45: ## Time and event ordering — NEW 46: 47: For a duration created in initiative, record the relevant original regular start/end marker. Existing effects keep that **virtual marker**, repeating every six seconds if the creature leaves or changes initiative. Reordering, rerolling, extra turns, death/removal or leaving/reentering initiative never restart an existing duration. New effects use the current schedule. Source death still ends concentration, a source-dependent aura/tether or an effect explicitly ending on death. 48: 49: - A bare hostile target-bound **N rounds** ends at the Nth future regular target-end marker. An in-progress target turn's current end does not count. 50: - A bare self/source buff, ally-targeted buff or source-controlled aura **N rounds** ends at the Nth future source-start marker. Explicit target-turn, pulse or absolute timing overrides this default. Activation during source turn 1 for three rounds ends at source turn 4's start. 51: - **Next turn** means the next future regular turn of the named creature, not the current turn's still-upcoming end. An unqualified next turn refers to source/user under the existing shared rule. Explicit “this turn” can include the current turn. 52: - An outside-initiative bare N-round effect instead gets an absolute application-time + N×6 seconds deadline. Entering initiative preserves that deadline. 53: - Explicit periodic profiles retain their registered clocks and tick counts. Never create a second timer merely because a generic duration rule also exists. 54: 55: The [exact GPT review](../../../../docs/audit/2026-10-08/full-game/GPT_COMBAT_EXACT_FINDINGS.md) confirms ally-buff source clocks and excluding the current turn for explicit next-turn endpoints. The helper excludes a specific current-end marker only if that marker would otherwise be selected: application during a turn at time1 with end3 excludes3 and selects9; application at/after3 also selects9, without erroneously skipping again to15. 56: 57: At a regular turn's **start**, resolve in this order: 58: 59: 1. Expire start-ending effects and apply immediate state changes from those expirations. 60: 2. Refresh Reaction; tick regular-start recharge/non-action clocks. 61: 3. Resolve already-due absolute state recovery if now legal, including a previously blocked Stable recovery deadline. 62: 4. If still alive, dying and at 0 HP with death saves, make that turn's death save. 63: 5. Resolve other start effects and periodic events in application order. 64: 6. Begin voluntary turn decisions. 65: 66: At **end**, resolve triggered effects, due damage, saves and maintenance before end-ending effects expire; then the slot finishes. No extra turn creates another regular marker. The caller must not reuse a resolved marker. 67: 68: For simultaneous effects, earlier application timestamp precedes later. For simultaneous components of one source, its printed internal order controls; otherwise use stable application sequence, with source/mechanical ID as a final tie-break. Record that sequence when creating the effects. Beneficial/harmful labels do not change priority. Periodic family arbitration happens once per family/marker before its damage, retaining the family registry's consumed loser ticks. 69: 70: Examples: dying at 0 rolls its death save before ordinary start-turn healing; a fatal death save is not undone by ordinary healing. Stable at 0 skips the save, then start-turn damage can break Stable. A natural 20 can restore 1 HP before a due Bleed drops it to 0 as a new drop event. If Decay expires while a Stable recovery deadline is already due, the old deadline resolves once without rerolling recovery time. 71: 72: ## Standard Actions 73: 74: | Action | Procedure | 75: |---|---| 76: | Attack | Make the attacks legally granted by class and weapon. Speed 0 grants none. Load measures burden; Extraction Class measures danger and logistics. Apply actual capacity, custody, hands, harnesses, checks and durability.     - generic: "168: Keep **visibility**, **known location**, and **line of effect** separate. Seeing through a barrier does not itself permit attacking through it. Best cover only: half +2 AC/DEX saves; three-quarters +5; total prevents direct targeting through that obstruction. Never add +2 and +5. For areas, evaluate line/cover from the area's origin to each target; total cover there excludes a creature unless the effect explicitly travels around/through the obstruction. The origin point itself must be legally targetable. 169: 170: Within normal ranged range, no range penalty; beyond normal through long, disadvantage; beyond long, illegal. Ranged attacks also have disadvantage when within5ft of a non-Incapacitated hostile that can see the attacker, even if the attacker cannot see that hostile. All advantage/disadvantage follows the single cancellation rule. 171: 172: NEW **Hide**: permitted only against observers unable to see you clearly or where a feature permits. Roll DEX Stealth once, record natural die and total. Against each observer's passive DC `10 + WIS Perception modifier including applicable proficiency`, use the Core four-degree task system. Meet DC succeeds before natural1/20 degree adjustment; Success/Critical Success establishes Hidden for that observer. No unsourced passive ±5 adjustment for advantage/disadvantage. Actual Search rolls use their normal modifiers. 173: 174: Hidden means that observer cannot see you and does not know your location. Clear sight or learning your position ends Hidden for that observer. Moving between hiding places can retain Hidden only if no clear sight/revealing event occurs. A failed Hide cannot simply be rerolled unchanged; a materially new hiding position, concealment, distraction, sight-breaking event or express reroll feature is required. Hidden is not a generic immunity or a free stealth roll for every move. 175: 176: **Search** Action: WIS Perception for sensory detection; INT Investigation for appropriate traces/deduction; Core Arcana/Engineering where relevant. Directly locating a Hidden creature defaults to Perception against its recorded final Stealth total as a static DC, using task degrees/natural shifts. Success reveals location only to the searcher and ends Hidden for that observer, without automatically granting sight. Communicate normally to inform others. Search can be repeated on later turns by paying the Action. 177: 178: Attacks against unseen targets have disadvantage; unseen attackers have advantage, subject to the **Core Invisible exception**: once its position is known, Invisible's own attack modifiers end for that observer and cannot immediately return solely because it remains Invisible. Independent darkness, smoke, blindness or hiding can still supply modifiers. 179: 180: Resolve an attack using pre-attack information; afterward reveal location to observers able to perceive its evidence. If an unseen target's location is unknown, do not directly choose it for a creature-target effect. A weapon/projectile can instead attack a suspected space: wrong space automatically misses; correct space uses unseen rules. A “creature you can see” requirement is never met by position knowledge alone. Area effects can target legal points under their own rules. 181: 182: ## Scope and validation 183: 184: [Reference checks](COMBAT_PLAYTEST_VALIDATION.json) exercise selected budgets, clocks and edge cases. They do not implement map visibility, inventory, feature legality, complete event dispatch, all maneuvers or a live application. No claim of encounter balance or human playtest completion follows from a passing report. 185: 186: `start_life_sequence` receives already-resolved/arbitrated HP events; it does not perform condition-family arbitration, cure processing, trigger generation, duration expiry, target legality, defense calculations or general simultaneous-event dispatch. `hide_results` answers only whether Hidden is established, collapsing the two success degrees and two failure degrees; it does not retain the full task-degree record. The Reaction helper models the ordinary resource, not additional Reaction grants printed on specific cards. 187: 188: Remaining work includes cargo terminology/capacity/durability; detailed movement/environment/chases; area templates and sensory abilities; summons/boss full-turn examples; all-source class/spell semantic interactions and specific resource grants; and the whole-game ledger's other acceptance gates. ---BODY first100--- # Bodies equipment and carrying **REVIEWED — authored tabletop body, equipment, carrying and continuous meld procedures.** New integration mechanics remain explicitly provisional; runtime implementation is pending. These procedures connect the [Druid form rules](FORM_TEMP_HP_PLAYTEST.md) and [transformation spells](TRANSFORMATION_SPELL_PLAYTEST.md). They apply to the same character, HP record and physical belongings throughout a change. They grant no additional forms, spell access, actions or resources. The approved books remain preserved. ## Entering and changing forms Before paying for a voluntary change, choose a legal form and size, check its space at your current position, and identify any equipment that must be dropped. If the destination or required drops are illegal, the change does not begin and spends nothing. Otherwise pay the source's normal costs and resolve its actual entry effects once. A successful hostile or forced transformation cannot be prevented merely by excess gear: resolve it under its source rules, then reconcile excess equipment by keeping it external, dropping it or recording pending placement. Other source requirements still apply. Your Druid form uses your own HP, ability scores, saves, proficiency and conditions. It supplies only its printed anatomy and features. Speech does not imply hands: none of the 26 current Druid profiles grants ordinary hand-based equipment use. Of the 12 spell profiles, only Humanoid grants hands. Each profile retains its exact size and movement permissions. | Change | Equipment procedure | |---|---| | Root body to first Druid form | Record and meld the equipment physically worn, held or carried by your body, including carried containers and their contents. | | Druid form to another Druid form | Keep the same original melded equipment. End the former form's dependent effects and start the new form under its normal rules. Reconcile newly acquired external items with the new anatomy. | | A successful spell replaces a Druid or spell form | Close the former transformation and capture the legitimate resulting gear under the new spell's meld rules in one continuous change. There is no moment when you can use the returning equipment. | | Shapechange changes its current spell profile | Keep compatible external items. Drop incompatible external items; they do not join the original melded equipment. | | Suppression | Follow the spell's suppression procedure. It is not an ending or a fresh capture of equipment. Original melded gear stays inaccessible. | | Final reversion, expiry or forced ending | Return to root anatomy, end the source's dependent effects and reconcile surviving equipment as described below. | The continuing meld record across Druid-to-Druid changes is a **new provisional integration rule**. It prevents shifting from opening an equipment-use window between forms. A spell replacement retains the already-reviewed close-and-capture procedure. ## What melds Record where each item was before the first change: worn in a particular slot, held in a particular hand, or carried in a particular container or on the body. Ownership alone is insufficient. A sword stored in a wagon stays in the wagon when its owner becomes a Hawk. A legitimately borrowed sword in your actual custody can meld and return normally; the lender remains its owner. Changing ownership metadata does not itself move an inaccessible physical item. A backpack and each of its contents remain distinct items. Count each item's own weight or Load once; a container does not erase its contents. Melded items remain in your custody but are inaccessible. You cannot withdraw or add contents, use components, consume supplies, fire ammunition or activate equipment through the meld. Melding never repairs damage, reloads a weapon, restores charges, replenishes supplies or creates a replacement item. Destruction, consumption and legitimate transfers remain real. Keep the current condition and quantity of each actual item. ## Using equipment in a form | Item or effect | Rule | |---|---| | Original armor and shield | Melded and unusable. Exclude their worn or held AC benefits from the form's normal AC. | | Held weapon, tool, focus or material component | Requires actual access and the appropriate anatomy. A form without hands receives no general permission to use it. | | Effect requiring an item to be worn, held or equipped | Suspended while the item is melded. | | Effect requiring only possession or attunement | May continue only when its exact rule is independent of the lost anatomy and physical use. An unspecified magic bonus is not automatically operative. | | Newly acquired armor, harness, collar or container | Must fit the actual size and anatomy and have a legal way to remain attached or carried. Hands are required for held items, not automatically for every wearable item. | | Newly acquired external gear when the form changes | Remains external if compatible. Otherwise use the normal drop procedure. It does not silently join an existing meld record. | The possession-versus-use distinction and individual compatibility checks are **new provisional defaults**. A specific item or form can supply an explicit exception. Do not infer one from a creature's name, appearance or ability to speak. Item-specific compatibility across the full equipment catalog remains to be authored. ## Returning equipment Resolve a final reversion in this order: 1. Keep current external items that still exist and remain legally equipped or carried by the root body. 2. Return each surviving melded item that remains in the character's legitimate custody to its recorded former slot or container, if available and legal. 3. If an external item now occupies that slot, leave it there. Return the former item as carried inventory if there is a legal place and sufficient capacity. 4. Drop an item that cannot be carried or equipped at the current position or the nearest legal adjacent item position. 5. If no legal drop position exists, record that item's placement as pending. It continues to exist; it is not duplicated, destroyed or used to displace another creature. Current external equipment taking priority over a former slot is a **new provisional integration rule**. A returned sword may go into available carried storage instead of replacing a sword you actually acquired and equipped during the transformation. Both retain their current condition and ownership. Destroyed, consumed, physically transferred, lost or otherwise unavailable items never reappear. An item in another custodian's possession cannot return merely because you still claim ownership of it. Someone else's legal ownership does not prevent an intact borrowed item from returning to your continuing legitimate custody. ## Placement and involuntary endings A transformation does not grant movement. A voluntary size change keeps the same spatial anchor and must fit before payment. Preserve the existing footprint on a same-size change when possible. A mandatory ending still occurs if the root anatomy does not fit. First try its normal space at the same position, then a legal squeeze under the [movement rules](COMBAT_ACTION_PLAYTEST.md). If neither fits, record a placement conflict. The **new provisional placement procedure** permits only a nearest legal adjacent placement in the same unobstructed region. It cannot pass through a wall, closed barrier or occupied space. Choose the minimum displacement that fits the whole body; among ties, preserve the greatest overlap with its previous footprint. If still tied, the Director uses a fixed neutral board order, not a tactical choice by the affected player. If no adjacent candidate fits, the conflict remains until the obstruction is removed or a legal relocation effect resolves it. Do not search farther afield. This mandatory normalization spends no movement and triggers no opportunity attack, charge distance, movement benefit or other movement rider. It changes no remaining movement allowance and grants no invented crushing damage. It does not permit a voluntary form change to bypass its preflight. Track actual height and terrain. Losing flight while unsupported starts ordinary falling from the current location; reversion never moves the creature directly to the ground. Ending a burrowing form underground does not move the creature to the surface. Master of Vermin's permission to pass through Tiny gaps changes clearance, not its printed size. Use the actual space of the expressed anatomy: | Size | Space | On a grid of 2.5-foot subcells | |---|---|---| | Tiny | 2.5 by 2.5 feet | 1 subcell | | Small | 5 by 5 feet | 2 by 2 subcells | | Medium | 5 by 5 feet | 2 by 2 subcells | | Large | 10 by 10 feet | 4 by 4 subcells | V6 explicitly supplies Tiny and Medium space. Small and Large complete this bounded table provisionally; the supplied V6 passage does not separately list them. Size category still matters even when two sizes occupy the same space. Squeezing changes clearance and its penalties; it does not change size, carrying capacity or weight. Exact Tiny occupancy requires subcells or equivalent measurement, not treating Tiny as Medium. ## Body weight and conditions **New provisional rule:** a creature-form transformation preserves the body's existing base physical weight unless an exact rule changes it. Size alone does not change weight. A 180-pound character therefore remains 180 pounds as a Tiny Hawk or Large Bear. This is a magical transformation rule, not a claim about the weight of ordinary animals. It applies equally to the current 26 Druid and 12 creature-form spell profiles. Record base body weight separately from equipment and condition effects. Petrified multiplies current body weight by ten once; it does not rewrite the base weight or multiply repeatedly for multiple applications. Ending or changing a form does not cure that condition. Temporary Druid form ends under its existing Incapacitated rule, then Petrified continues to apply to the reverted body. An anatomy permitted to persist keeps its own base weight and the same condition overlay. True Polymorph object anatomy uses its actual documented output weight. Its output ceiling remains the lower of current effective input weight and earliest recorded origin weight. Include a current applicable weight-changing condition in the input, but never rebase the origin ceiling upward. Equipment, casualty Load and estimated pounds do not establish material available for a transformation. Creature-origin object anatomy has no independent sale, salvage or harvest value. For another creature, mount, floor or platform supporting you, count your effective body weight plus all actual equipment in your custody, including melded items, once. For your own inventory capacity, count what you carry, not your own body weight. Conditions change only the items or body their exact rules affect. ## Carrying and Load Use pounds for actual weight and Load for inventory burden. Extraction Class describes cargo danger and attention. A casualty's Load describes the difficulty of transporting a body; it is not the body's weight. These quantities answer different questions and never convert automatically into each other. The following is a **new provisional reconciliation** of V6 physical handling with the staged Gameplay Loop inventory rules. It does not promote the historical Gameplay Loop file into the approved books. V6's optional encumbrance variant remains preserved as an alternative; do not add its penalties to this Load procedure. | Carrier size | Physical carry limit in pounds | Push, drag or lift limit in pounds | Inventory capacity in Load | Casualty Load for a body of this size | |---|---|---|---|---| | Tiny | Strength score × 7.5 | Strength score × 15 | Strength score ÷ 2 | 1.5 | | Small | Strength score × 15 | Strength score × 30 | Maximum of 0 and Strength score − 2 | 3 | | Medium | Strength score × 15 | Strength score × 30 | Strength score | 6 | | Large | Strength score × 30 | Strength score     - heading "2. Complete common execution contract" [level=2]
    - heading "BRP01-COMMON-01" [level=3]
    - paragraph:
      - text: These are
      - strong: scoped creature-action completions
      - text: ", not new general combat rules. Printed HP, AC, abilities, CR, type, every printed action and the entire original dossier text remain unchanged."
    - paragraph:
      - text: Each anatomical melee action below is physical and nonspell, with
      - strong: 5-foot reach
      - text: ", measured between actual occupied body boundaries using a clear contact/line-of-effect witness. Pressure Wave has its own declared emitter and range. The actors must actually perceive and legally target their recipients."
    - paragraph:
      - text: Validate before commitment and again before the particular outcome. A known illegal attempt spends nothing. After legal commitment, a genuinely authorized interruption can invalidate the pending outcome; the spent Action or attack selection is not refunded, and that outcome is not retargeted.
      - strong: No new interruption window or free Reaction is supplied. Ordinary Ready remains trigger-first.
    - paragraph: A standalone action spends one real Action. A permitted Multiattack spends one Action once, resolves its two selections in order and revalidates each selection independently. Each performed selection consumes its slot even on a miss or successful recipient save. An unusable remaining slot does not become another Action or a refund.
    - paragraph: Use the actual printed attack modifiers. Do not infer proficiency from CR, owner level or saving-throw proficiency. Attack natural 1 misses; natural 20 hits and doubles damage dice, not constants. Ordinary saves are binary and have no generic natural-1/natural-20 exception.
    - paragraph:
      - text: Each genuine damage event rolls separately and retains its original source actor as dealer. Create its source-qualified damage root and preserve any
      - strong: actually qualifying creation-time Null flag
      - text: . Transfer does not replay the action or control rider.
    - paragraph:
      - text: Apply actual defenses, temporary HP, normal HP, life/Wound/zero-state consequences and concentration checks in their accepted order. Wound thresholds apply
      - strong: per packet
      - text: ", not to summed Multiattack damage. These actions do not gain Maiming or an unprinted Wound exemption."
    - paragraph: An on-hit control save or post-damage movement applies only while the same recipient remains an eligible creature. Damage prevention does not automatically prevent an independently admitted control rider. No action manufactures equipment damage, loot, recharge, another harvest slot or a new corpse.
    - paragraph: Forced movement spends no voluntary movement and provokes no ordinary opportunity attack. Sweep the whole body and stop at the first obstruction to legal placement. It grants no passage through another body, collision damage or Hover. Broken reach ends the applicable grapple; loss of support uses the existing Falling contract.
    - paragraph: Retain action, subattack, root and condition-source identifiers so repeated delivery cannot duplicate a slot, packet, push or grapple.
    - paragraph:
      - text: The supplied scene bodies and schedules are
      - strong: synthetic fixture inputs
      - text: ", not authenticated campaign actors or a decision about distributed formations. They do not settle BV3-16."

    - heading "3. BV3-04 — complete Pressure Wave proposal" [level=2]
    - heading "BRP01-PW-01" [level=3]
    - paragraph:
      - strong: "Preserved:"
      - text: one target, DC15, 3d8 force and a control effect.
    - paragraph:
      - strong: "NEW selected completion:"
    - paragraph:
      - strong: Target and geometry.
      - text: Choose one perceived, legally targetable creature within
      - strong: 30 feet
      - text: of a declared emitter point on the Sentinel’s actual body. Require an unobstructed line to an actual interior target point. Record those positions at discharge. The emitter cannot move outside the body to extend range. This action uses Euclidean three-dimensional distance; that does not rewrite the global grid convention.
    - paragraph:
      - strong: Category and cost.
      - text: Pressure Wave is a
      - strong: magical nonspell native force action
      - text: . It costs one real Action or, by this expressly new local permission, one selection in this Sentinel’s printed two-selection Multiattack. Each Wave has its own save, dice and root.
    - paragraph: It adds no RP, V/S/F, ammunition, concentration or recharge cost. Actual source-qualified suppression can prevent its magical output. Ordinary spell-only Counterspell does not acquire permission to counter a nonspell.
    - paragraph:
      - strong: Resolution.
      - text: After actual interruptions and revalidation, the recipient makes
      - strong: STR DC15
      - text: .
    - list:
      - strong: "Success:"
      - text: no damage and no push; do not roll damage.
      - strong: "Failure:"
      - text: roll
      - strong: 3d8 force
      - text: once. After its damage and actual life/concentration consequences, push a still-eligible living recipient
      - strong: up to 10 feet
      - text: along the fixed direction from the discharge emitter through the declared target point.
    - paragraph: Normalize that direction. A zero-length emitter-to-target direction is illegal at declaration. Sweep the target’s whole body, stopping at placement obstructions. An actually available push-reduction feature, such as a previously paid Brace, applies within its scope. No size exemption or additional save is invented.
    - paragraph:
      - text: The push is instantaneous. It adds
      - strong: no Prone, Stunned, restraint, persistent zone or repeated tick
      - text: . Completed damage and movement are not undone by later suppression or source removal. Removal before discharge can prevent the pending discharge. A now-dead recipient is not moved as a newly invented creature effect.
    - paragraph:
      - strong: "Containment Pulse remains separate:"
      - text: area, STR DC15, 5d6 force, half on success, Recharge 5–6 and its printed charge-up. Its missing full area and the broader recharge/formation/Multiattack questions remain for their own parts. Part01 does not grant it a Pressure Wave slot or treat it as the same ability.
    - paragraph:
      - strong: Reason and tradeoff.
      - text: This supplies a useful resisted displacement without importing an area blast or additional condition. Success-zero follows the printed “succeed … or take” structure. STR, range, magical category, push distance and local Multiattack substitution are
      - strong: new proposals
      - text: ", not recovered facts."
